7. **Research honesty.** Verify current facts online, never from memory. Every claim: URL +
   access date. Separate evidence from opinion; say what would falsify each recommendation. A
   recommendation without license + cost is incomplete. An unverified claim lives in a register
   marked UNVERIFIED — refuted by default — and is never quietly promoted.
8. **Handoff and single writer.** On completion: `plan.py close <ID>` — the status, the ≤8-line
   handoff (what exists, deviations, **every finding you measured and chose not to act on, with
   the reason**, what the next teammate needs, the model you ran on, `Next: M<N>-<id>`) and the
   register lines, in one call; detail in `tasks/<ID>.md`, the summary atop your report. A flag
   aimed at a later task is written into that task's `Carried
   flags:` now. Change nothing else — except that a CHECK block (V, TA, TL, the TW scribe) may
   move blocks it did not write, move only, to keep the plan runnable top to bottom (E8).
   Parallel-safe tasks never edit the plan (§6). Out-of-scope bugs are filed as D blocks (E1),
   never silently fixed.
9. **Docs and context discipline.** If your change alters a contract stated in a `docs/agent/`
   page you read, update the page in the same task. If it adds a route, target, scene or case that
   any harness enumerates (allowlists, capture routes, probe registries, the routes manifest),
   update that harness in the same task — a stale allowlist silently breaks the gate. A rename
   greps for every other script that hard-codes the old shape. Never prove your work by
   re-reading files into context; the `Verify:` scope is the check.
10. **Lead action items.** Company facts, funding, outreach, legal duties, store enrolment,
    billing rulings: never a coding `Deliver:` — append them to the tracker's worklist.

**Engineering taste (we all follow it):** the real fix, not the patch — interim hacks only when
labelled interim, then replaced wholesale · interfaces are contracts: if one is wrong, stop and
report, never adapt around it · one implementation attempt + self-verify, then report and stop —
still red after one fix round → log the defect and close out · bug repros become permanent
regression cases · a flaky-infrastructure symptom sighted a second time becomes an owned D block
in that same edit; a third sighting with no owned block is a V finding · scope creep is parked
explicitly · the test suite only grows — coverage is never dropped to move faster; coverage of
retired surface retires with it, and that is bookkeeping · a second implementation of a primitive
the project already owns is a defect.

---

## §5 Dynamic blocks — E1–E10

Dynamic types, each with a counter in the Pipeline state: `D<n>` debug one precise bug (BUILD) ·
`V<n>` validation on a scope (CHECK) · `Q<n>` question for the lead (LEAD answers; unattended or
orchestrated runs only) · `TI<n>` lead interview on a pivot (PLAN + LEAD answers, E9) · `TJ<n>`
direction ruling (PLAN + LEAD answers) · `TV<n>` UI/UX pass (BUILD) · `TC<n>` compaction (MOVE) ·
`TR<n>` test-time pass (BUILD) · `TM<n>` rating pass (PLAN, §0).
Templates: §14.3. Every block is filed with `plan.py append`, which places it, writes its Flow row
and increments its counter in one call; its filer writes its heading's rating (§0, §3.2).

- **E1 · A defect you find is a D block you write.** In your scope → fix it yourself (no D). Out of
  scope → a D earns filing when it passes three tests: **(i)** it is a defect — the tree gives a
  wrong or unauditable result today (a mandated behaviour you dislike is a contract question, a
  suspicion is a note); **(ii)** it is measured, and the measurement is in the block; **(iii)** it
  is sized like any BUILD block, split at filing. You write the spec — its heading's rating
  included, the ladder's `(usual)` rung unless §0's rubric says otherwise — and never build it.
  It is placed right after your block by default, or where it will run correctly (inside a block
  already scheduled to touch that surface, or before the artifact it would rewrite) under an
  ordering note. If it blocks you, set `Blocks:` and go
  `BLOCKED-D<n>`. If ≥5 D are open (E6), log it in your handoff instead — the triage-V converts it.
  A flaky symptom sighted twice is a defect found. **A finder who files nothing says so** — the
  handoff's not-acted-on line names which test each finding failed.
- **E2 · Planned validation.** TG inserts a V after each lot plus a final V before the doc task. A
  lot has no upper bound: how many T blocks exist is decided by §2.1's tests alone — more, smaller
  blocks are always preferred, and a lot of sixteen micro blocks that land clean is the target
  shape. The V that closes a lot exists to come back with zero D.
- **E3 · Reactive validation.** A handoff recording a deviation that touches a shared file or a
  contract → a targeted V on that scope, unless one is already TODO. Two D closed on the same file
  → targeted V. At most one reactive V per scope between two planned tasks; a second consecutive
  V on the same scope still finding defects → ask the lead.
- **E4 · V findings.** Each defect a V finds becomes its own D (E1's three tests, files named or
  marked unverified), appended right after the V — never a fix inside the V.
- **E5 · Open decision.** A decision the contract doesn't settle that clears Rule 6's bars → a
  question (§12); fails a bar → sane default, declared, continue. Never a question on something
  the lead already stated.
- **E6 · Anti-explosion.** ≥5 open D → stop spawning; a **triage-V** (CHECK, next task) merges
  duplicates, prioritizes, closes what should close. The same bug reopened after two closed D →
  ask; two failed fix attempts in one session → a fresh session with the trail (§0).
- **E7 · Execution order.** Awaited answers → D blocking the next task → next planned task →
  non-blocking D → V at their insertion points. The file is ordered by execution, never by id: an
  id records when a defect was found, the file records what to do next.
- **E8 · Append-only, placed where it runs, runnable top to bottom.** Never delete or renumber a
  block; cancelled → `N/A` + one line. Every dynamic block sits where it will run; when creation
  order and run order disagree, run order wins under one ordering note. A CHECK block (V, TA, TL,
  the TW scribe) may move blocks it did not write — move only (`plan.py move` refuses if a byte
  would change), every move named in the note and the handoff. Archiving a closed block to `plan_archive*.md` is not deletion: ids,
  order and content survive, the stub stays in place.
- **E9 · Significant pivot.** A direction change that upends the current contract or architecture
  → a dynamic TI, placed before the task that consumes its findings, eliciting the mechanics of
  the new direction — never whether to take it. Its bookkeeping is the TJ (§2.6). A change big
  enough for a fresh milestone opens a from-scratch plan with its own seed TI.
- **E10 · Journals.** Defects observed during the live walk land as numbered one-line entries
  (`TW2-W1…`) in `reports/walk<k>.md`, and the lead's review-page remarks land verbatim in
  `reports/review<k>.md` — both append-only, no fixes during the sitting. A walk's own assertions
  are journalled and falsified like any artifact; a walk that reports no wrong assertion has
  probably not checked. Afterwards **one triage pass**: collect first, measure once against the
  code, put the forks to the lead in blocks of ≤4, then file the D blocks batched and placed where
  they run — never one full ceremony per remark.

---

## §6 Sequentiality and parallel safety

- **Default: strictly sequential.** One task in flight; each assumes the previous DONE. Tasks
  touching the same files are sequential even when logically independent; contract-amending
  tasks always are. A plan may declare **STRICTLY LINEAR** in one line: no ∥ sets, no exceptions.
- **Parallel-safe sets** may be declared per plan with explicit file-disjointness reasoning
  (`∥ set-X`). Parallel tasks never edit the plan: each puts its ≤8-line handoff atop its own
  report and one sequential writer pastes them in. No task reads a same-wave peer's in-flight
  files. Shared codegen, cache and import steps run solo. A set whose blocks all write to one new
  schema or checker runs one of them first, alone, until its check is GO; the rest start after.
- **Wind-down — stop first, write second:** on any wrap-up, stop every background process first,
  then write the resume-exact handoff; a task wound down at the lead's word records its state in
  `tasks/<ID>.md`, dated, and resumes from it. **Never idle-wait** for a notification you will
  not receive; run sub-work synchronously or poll evidence files.
- **One toolkit, one owner.** A toolkit shared by concurrent milestones or sibling tracks has one
  owner milestone, named in each sharing plan's Repo facts: only the owner's blocks edit it; the
  others file a flag into the owner's plan; a bump checks every sibling's adoption.

---

## §7 Orchestrator mode — opt-in, exact words « you are an orchestrator »

- **O1 Dispatch, don't do.** Spawn one fresh sub-agent per task id, its type from the heading and
  its model and level the ones the lead named for the batch — "each heading's rating" is such a
  naming (§0). Agents obey §4; Rule 0 forbids them sub-spawning. An agent with no sub-agents
  (§B.3) does not orchestrate: the lead launches one session per task.
- **O2 Read only the plan** — never deliverables, reports, logs or repo files. An agent returns
  its ≤8-line handoff.
- **O3 Single writer.** You alone edit the plan during your batch (`plan.py`): paste handoffs,
  set statuses. Verification stays shallow (the deliverable path exists); depth belongs to V.
- **O4 Ordering.** Parallel dispatch only for plan-marked ∥ sets, never assumed — ask before any
  batch. Gate order (§2.1) holds. BLOCKED or a critical question returns to the lead.
- **O5 Notes.** Bugs or questions not yours to settle → `- Orchestrator notes (<date>):` after
  the last dispatched block, `BUG:` / `QUESTION:` bullets.
- **O6 Your handoff.** ≤8 lines: tasks DONE/BLOCKED + a pointer to the notes.
- **O7 A block an agent files runs next** — before you resume your own queue, recursively; a
  defect found mid-chain is not built upon for four more blocks.
- **O8 The status page is a two-way channel.** `tools/pb/status_page.py serve` (extracted with
  the toolkit, annex §A.6) — on the lead's private network when the lead is away (address
  resolved at run time, never written into a tracked file), its own port, phone-first: every task
  in the queue in run order, each task's status and running time, an **attention list**
  (decisions you took without the lead, things to commit, things to know), and questions. A
  non-critical question is posted and the run continues on a declared default; a **critical one — proceeding would be unsafe or unrecoverable: a push, anything
  destructive or paid, a freeze — halts that block until answered.** Poll the page until the run
  ends (`status_page.py poll`): a message posted while you were done polling is the incident this
  rule closes. Run-scoped grants (a push, GPU, steering a V) never carry to the next run.

---

## §8 Verification and the user gate

- **Stance: every claim is wrong until it survives attack** — against the real running artifact,
  never from memory. It applies to what is claimed, not to what is being replaced: a V verifies
  the surface that survives the milestone and never opens a defect for retired behaviour (§2.6).
- **Verifiers report; they don't fix.** A V reads handoffs, log summaries and the code slice,
  renders `CONFORMANT | DEFECTS(n) | NOT PROVEN (<why>)` with `CONFIRMED / REFUTED / OVERSTATED /
  UNVERIFIABLE` per finding, files a D per defect (E4), names its blind spot and its box
  (`CONFORMANT (static — no live run, on <box>)`). **A phase-closing V also keeps the plan in
  shape:** every DONE block of the phase is a stub (`plan.py stub`, ≤10 blocks; more → append a
  `TC<n>`), the header is within its budget and margin (§11), the complete loop runs once and the fast numbers are recorded (§2.3).
- **A test must fail under the bug.** A green run proves a fix only if the same test is red on the
  unfixed code. A declared guard, filter, scanner, exclusion or contract clause ships **red-armed
  in the same task**: its scope declares the planted bug and `verify.py --redarm` proves it red —
  injected in memory or in a scratch copy, never by editing a source or data file; the loop runs
  the plants of every scope a block added or changed; a diagnostic is evidence only once its branches are
  shown reachable; widening a guard is not wiring a value; an exclusion fails toward red. A
  `Verify:` that cannot fail is banned at authoring; a check that cannot pass on the artifact it
  runs against is equally useless.
- **Real data, or it is not proven.** Synthetic fixtures alone → `NOT PROVEN (synthetic)`; every
  scope item's real-data proof is scheduled inside its own lot's V. Fixture fidelity: show the
  fixture has the real corpus's shape before reading a green case as evidence. Mock fidelity: a
  double never accepts a call the real dependency would reject. Anything compiled, packaged or
  frozen gets its own boot smoke of the packaged artifact; a code-read is `NOT PROVEN (source
  only)`.
- **A green runner is a claim about an environment.** Every verdict states its box, and every
  loop record the box and interpreter it ran on; a gate is not green until it is green on every
  machine in play, or the handoff says which one is owed. A scope that needs one box declares it
  (`box:` in the manifest) and reads `owed on <box>` elsewhere, never NO-GO; the loop budget is
  stated per box. Check the ruler before tuning: before any ≥3-iteration tuning loop, one
  independent check that the
  fixture matches contract. Adversarial passes report their kill rate — an all-CONFIRMED table is
  itself a finding. A read-through verdict is labelled read-through.
- **Human-run tiers run per lot.** When a tier (e2e, device, GPU) is the lead's alone to run, the
  plan schedules one run of it inside each lot's V window; a lot does not close with its
  human-run tier unexercised — a backlog of unrun acceptance surfaces as a wall of D's later.
- **`TV` · the UI/UX pass (BUILD) — how the lead's eye lands.** Once per phase that adds or
  changes screens: capture every screen and state the phase touched with the capture harness
  (`tools/pb/capture_web.mjs`, annex §A.5, for web stacks — deterministic viewport, network-idle
  + ready selector, the mobile-width overflow pass; game engines feed their own captures), then
  look at every capture yourself: a wall of text, a janky or confusing flow, an overflow, a
  state that reads wrong — each becomes a D block (E1), never a fix inside the TV. Build the page
  (`tools/pb/review_page.py`, annex §A.4) with only the captures that need the lead's eyes, each
  with your note on why, and serve it; wait while the lead pages through at their own pace —
  remarks are saved verbatim, keyed by capture, to `reports/review<k>.md`; then one triage pass
  (E10), then stop the server (§6). What a static capture cannot show — feel, animation, timing, sound, haptics,
  device-only behaviour, a signature limb — is listed once in Rules and marked `NOT PROVEN
  (static capture)` by the TV.
- **`TW` · the live walk — one per milestone, the user gate.** The lead drives the real increment
  from the launchers; one CHECK scribe journals (E10), observation only, no fixes mid-walk; a walk
  or any human-driven script is handed over one step at a time. An increment that won't boot is a
  NO-GO defect, not a reason to skip. Approval withheld is a NO-GO with a journal, not an approval
  with a punch list. Every increment stays runnable end-to-end at every milestone, and **every
  runnable project ships OS-native start/stop launchers in its first milestone**
  (`tools/pb/launch.py` behind a double-click file) — the gate never requires memorizing a
  command. TP flags a plan with more than one live walk unless it names what a capture cannot
  show.

---

## §9 Contracts

- One `m<N>_contrat.md` per M/L milestone, authored by a PLAN task: every schema, endpoint,
  formula, UI string (in the product language, exact), design token and error message.
  **Done-when:** a builder can implement every downstream block from the contract alone.
- Amendments happen in place by PLAN tasks, marked with a prevailing tag (`[T7]`); frozen sections
  are marked FROZEN; a ratified clause sits beside the lead's verbatim words. Once its milestone
  closes, a contract is read-only; a later contract only adds, and says which passage it replaces.
- **No dead or unarmed surface at freeze.** A task that freezes a channel or endpoint greps for its
  producer and consumer; a clause that names its own enforcement is frozen only once that
  mechanism was proven red (§8).
- **The declared override — one line, not an essay.** A passage the milestone's own goal requires
  changing is declared, not escalated: one line in the current contract's §0 (passage · what
  replaces it · why the goal needs it), one mirror line in the plan's Superseded section, one in
  the handoff — three places, always, because self-judgment is the risk. Any type may declare one
  for a passage its own `Deliver:` requires; it covers a FROZEN section and a prior read-only
  contract alike (the closed file is never edited). Where the plan header reads `Changes by
  asking: every block`, a block may go further and propose a small durable change by asking (§0).
  **Asking stays mandatory** for a product fork the
  lead has not settled · a change outside the milestone's stated scope · §2.6 P2 · any override
  touching persisted data, a migration or an irreversible operation. A corrected number is a §0
  arithmetic correction, not a retirement.

---

## §10 Git and commit gates

- Agents never write git — no add, commit, checkout, restore, stash, reset, clean, push. Read-only
  git is always allowed: `status`, `diff`, `log`, `rev-parse`, `show`. Commit and branch
  decisions are the lead's.
- **The one exception: a bump's propagation run** (§15). After the lead's go, the propagation tool
  pulls each adopter (`--ff-only`), writes the new version, commits everything in one commit per
  repository (`git add -A`) and pushes when the lead said yes — never a force, a reset, a merge or
  a branch switch. In the canonical repository it runs no git write at all.
- Every measured number carries its SHA (`git rev-parse HEAD`, plus `git status --short` when the
  tree is dirty) so the claim is falsifiable.
- **Cadence:** commit and push at the end of every phase and every milestone — small, reviewable
  commits. Each phase's closing step is a `> **Commit gate (lead):**` block with the exact `git
  add` / `git commit -m "M<N> Phase X: <what>"` / `git push` commands, one per block. Never more
  than one phase (~6 tasks) beyond the last confirmed commit; the register's `Outstanding commit
  gates` line is checked at every gate. Push before the user gate; a gate rejection becomes
  layered fix commits, never a force-push. Never `reset --hard`, never repo-wide `clean`.
- **A box trip is not a commit.** Under an OPT-C REMOTE lane a task may ship files to the named
  test box and run there; the two machines are reconciled to one commit at the task's end, the box
  never commits or pushes, and the handoff states both SHAs.

---
## §11 Context economy — what any one agent has to read

**No line count binds a `.md` artifact.** Density binds — densify because prose is loose, never
cut a fact to hit a number — and **structure** binds, because structure controls what an agent
reads per task. The header above the first task block is read by every agent on every task, so
it has a **budget: ≤600 lines, Pipeline state ≤60** (a plan may tune it in Rules). `plan.py
lint` reports the header size on every run and goes **red when a budget is exceeded by more
than the margin — 100 lines (`--tc-margin`; a plan may tune it in Rules) — and no `TC<n>` is
TODO**; an overage inside the margin is reported, never fired. Filing the `TC<n>` (one `plan.py
append`) discharges the red, and the `TC<n>` runs next — ahead of every other block, an adoption
chain included — and brings the header back within the budget itself; a bump inserts it first on
a plan it finds past the margin (§15). This is a trigger that moves structure, not a cap that
shaves facts.

- **`TC<n>` · compaction (MOVE — any model: the tool refuses a changed byte).** (i) Every DONE /
  N/A / DEFERRED block outside the current phase moves to `plan_archive*.md`, leaving the stub the
  tool writes — heading · `Status: … · archived by TC<n>, <date>`, the block's own date kept before
  it · one-line handoff · `Full block: <file> § <id>`; the Flow row stays. (ii) The Pipeline state
  becomes the register again — any entry that restates a handoff becomes a pointer; a fact that
  lives only there is salvaged into the register, never dropped. (iii) A line repeated in three
  or more blocks is hoisted to Rules; a Rules entry longer than ~3 lines moves to `m<N>_rules.md`
  leaving its index line, **never reworded**. (iv) Repo facts de-duplicated. Safety: every moved
  line is diffed byte-for-byte before replacement and the count reported; the tool refuses to
  touch a TODO block, renumber an id, or change a rule's wording. Triggers: the lint's red · a
  phase-closing V with more than ten blocks to stub · a bump that finds the header past the
  margin · the lead's word.
- **`TM<n>` · rating pass (PLAN, §0) — the same shape.** `plan.py lint` goes red at the plan's
  `Rating refresh:` count (default 5) of carried flags added to open BUILD blocks since the
  register's `Model ratings:` line (§0 says which count), while no `TM<n>` is TODO or IN PROGRESS;
  filing it (one `plan.py append`, by any agent) discharges the red, and it runs next, after a
  pending `TC<n>`. A phase-closing V whose record moves a class (§0's rule (4)) files one by hand —
  no count sees it. It moves a block up only by a rule it names (§0, no step up on a guess). It
  also carries the rating off
  any authoring or adoption block that would rate more than ~15 blocks in its own session, so no
  long session also carries a whole plan's ratings (the lead: "delegated when needed").
- **Rules as an index.** When a plan's Rules pass ~60 lines they become one line per rule (number
  · title · operative clause) and the full text — grants, derogations, the sizing bar, the
  REJECTED list — lives in `m<N>_rules.md`, binding as if it sat in the plan. Numbers are never
  reused across milestones, so citations survive. Agents open the annex at the numbers their block
  touches.
- **A task's detail lives in `tasks/<ID>.md`** — the normal path; the plan keeps the ≤8-line
  handoff. A warning that applies to every task is stated once in Rules, and so is a shape
  repeated in three or more blocks — a block cites a playbook rule instead of restating it;
  `plan.py lint` flags a line repeated in three or more blocks. A `Read:` line lists only the
  additional sources, and names a file over ~500 lines by section or line range, never whole —
  the lint warns on a whole-file name above that size, and a task that truly needs the whole
  file says why.
- **Compaction keeps the binding "considered and REJECTED (false economy)" list** (§2.1 TB); a
  plan line that is stricter than this playbook's wording is never de-duplicated away. A
  compaction diffs the facts it kept, not just the lines it moved.
- **A rule that must hold goes into a check.** A rule that must never be skipped, or that agents
  broke twice, moves from prose into `plan.py lint` or a harness scope — never into a settings file
  beside the instruction file: this file stays the only one. Claude Code's switch (§B.3) carries
  no rule: its plugin is an annex tool, its hook line generated like the instruction files. Once its check has held for a
  version, its prose leaves the per-task read and the check's message carries it. Moved in v12:
  the status words, a whole-file `Read:`, a line repeated in three blocks, the type tag, a
  `Deliver:` item no check names (`plan.py lint`); the red arm, an edit to a grading test, a write
  outside the block's type (`verify.py`).
- **Any other file splits by topic when it stops being navigable** — reports, contracts, annexes,
  docs, source. Code splits by cohesion: if you can't name the piece without "and", it isn't one.
  This file is read on demand, never per task.
- **Never bulk-copy a regenerable tree** (`models/`, `.venv/`, `data/`, `var/`) into a scratch copy
  — two sessions filled a disk that way. `tools/pb/scratch_copy.sh` (annex §A.7) does the
  sanctioned rsync-exclude and symlinks the environments back; name the path in your handoff; the copy's owner
  removes it.
- Offload routing:

  | Content | Goes to | Shape |
  |---|---|---|
  | Findings, research, evidence, rationale | `reports/<topic>.md`, one topic per file | dense; split by topic when unnavigable |
  | Verdicts, audits, red-team results | `reports/<topic>.md` | counts + failures |
  | Task detail, handoff record, dynamic-block bodies | `tasks/<TASK-ID>.md` | the normal home |
  | Raw command and test output | `logs/<TASK-ID>.log` · `logs/loop_times.jsonl` | never in chat, never in the plan |
  | Captures, review-page sets | `images/` (git-ignored) | per task / per `tv<k>` |
  | Build and measurement artifacts, corpora outputs | `output/` (untracked) | — |
  | Repo-wide facts, hazards, grants | the plan's Repo facts and Rules annex | dated, root-caused |

- Failures verbatim (never truncate an error frame); passes as a count. Handoffs ≤8 inline,
  self-run summaries ≤3, orchestrator bullets ≤2 — shapes, with a home for the detail.

---

## §12 Asking the lead

**The question tool is the channel** (§B.3 names it per agent; without one, numbered options in
your message and `IN PROGRESS (awaiting: …)`) — up to four questions per call, one decision per
question, your recommendation first, in **plain words**: simple words and simple explanations,
bullet points first, every id or term explained in the line that uses it — the same for every
handoff and summary the lead reads; batch related questions; resolve open forks before code is
written. A question that freezes a clause restates its consequence in one plain sentence
("if yes, X will never …"). If the turn must end on a question, `Status: IN PROGRESS (awaiting:
<question>)`. Zero mid-task questions across a milestone is the desired outcome — forks are
pre-resolved at gates. **Unattended and orchestrated runs** use the Q block (§14.3) or the status
page's question channel (§7 O8): a non-critical question continues on a declared default, a
critical one halts. Clear Rule 6's three bars first; below the bar, default and declare. A fact
the disk holds is probed, never asked. The lead's exact words for any direction land in the
consuming task's `Ask (verbatim):` field and are quoted, never restated; a paraphrase beside them
is labelled as your reading. A step only the lead can do — a walk, a look, a commit and push, an
unruled question — still open at the next gate (a TP, a `TE-V<N>`, a V) is put again there as a
live question with options, never left as a passive premise; unanswered across two milestones it
is escalated to the worklist with a dated line, never re-asked a third time. Billing, pricing and
client-facing decisions are
always the lead's. Ad-hoc lead-spawned tasks slot between planned tasks as D blocks (`DBG` when
the lead directed them) without reopening gates. An idea raised outside scope follows §2.6 P9. The
lead may pre-authorize a specific future self-service action, recorded and dated in the plan.
When the lead says "add that to the standing rules", an act-scoped grant becomes a Rules line in
the same edit; a superseded standing rule leaves a dated mirror line in Superseded, never a silent
deletion. A question the lead has answered the same way three times: the agent proposes it as a
standing answer, one Rules line; a gate the lead has overridden three times is proposed for
retirement.

---

## §13 Optional modules (activated at bootstrap or by a plan)

**OPT-A · Measurement anti-gaming** (optimization or measurement-heavy work)
- Scope fence: OPT-A governs work that improves a thing that stays — the baseline is the earlier
  version of the same artifact. It never applies to a declared rewrite or pivot (§2.6): no R-BASE
  against surface being deleted, no before/after whose "before" will not exist at close.
- Pre-registered survival criterion (research/ML): what counts as a surviving finding is fixed
  before any result exists, and checked for satisfiability against its own corpus at the moment it
  is frozen; a round carries a positive control, and a control that fails to reproduce the frozen
  baseline voids the round.
- Stage-1 skeptic (CHECK) prompted to refute each finding, defaulting to REFUTED on thin evidence;
  kill rate reported. Stage-2 acceptance: before/after beats the clean-tree baseline on the target
  and nothing regresses across the corpus, else reverted.
- Budget beside every point (resource, latency, device; floor vs measured peak). Two honest
  protocols → pre-declare the stricter, deliver it, show both.
- R-BASE: the "before" is transcribed from its recorded run at its SHA, never re-derived; no
  record → declare the baseline ABSENT. R-CORPUS: one frozen corpus (≥3 inputs incl. an edge case)
  for the milestone; a win on one favourable input is not a win. R-SEED: harness helpers assert
  their precondition is present. A bounded diagnostic inside a costly run records its anomaly and
  continues; it never aborts the measurement. Acceptance is absolute, never "no worse than the old
  one"; quality claims are falsifiable from saved artifacts alone.

**OPT-B · Show-off demo (`TD`)** — after the final V, a TD stages a loadable demo + ≤1-page note,
runs the render harness itself and iterates until non-janky before the lead sees it. Authoring
new content and re-verifying old content are two blocks.

**OPT-C · Standing self-run grants** — every grant states **scope · exclusions · ceiling** (a
dollar ceiling for anything metered, drawn per task and never merged across milestones). Lanes:
**GATES** (run, never edit, the milestone's own gate/probe/demo harnesses) · **DEBUG** (drive your
own artifact headless before handoff — execution, never acceptance) · **BUILD** (the whole local
compile+probe loop; granted means run — handing a granted block back is the failure this lane
closes) · **REMOTE** (ship files to the named test box, run the scope there, reconcile both
machines to one commit at task end, §10; a GPU there is shared when the run's memory fits, as §4
Rule 4's long-run rule says). A grant is a lane, never a licence: anything paid, a run over 10
minutes, windowed or foreground-stealing, or irreversible is asked every time. Grants buy
execution, never acceptance; no golden is produced
under a grant unless the plan says so; a hardware-bound test the box cannot run is
`[NOT RUN — owed on <box>]`.

**OPT-D · Real-data isolation** — real files live only in a git-ignored samples directory; never
commit real exports, never copy real rows into contracts, handoffs or code. Build against a
probable fake format behind one swap point; format-dependent items tagged provisional — removing
the tag requires the validating comparison to have run. The product's own UI/API is a leak
surface: never render the samples path or listings; a content filter covers every outbound string
(`tools/pb/content_gate.py`, annex §A.8, over the project's denylist runs as a scope). Purge logic targets an
enumerated allow-list, never `rmtree`; teardown of a shared database is by recorded id. Once
manual entry ships, the database stops being disposable: no task deletes or rebuilds it,
migrations are forward-only, anything touching persisted data asks. **TL · legal and data-basis
clearance (CHECK)** clears consent, retention, jurisdiction and licensing before any task consumes
real data, per track and per milestone, never inherited; it may be re-armed as a written trigger
(any DDL naming a `user`/`tenant` table voids it).

**OPT-E · Parallel product tracks** — a second product line in the same repo: its own tracker
(`milestones<T>.md`) stating only deltas, its own prefix (`2M0…`) and plan tree, the same gates
and close-out, its own TL; it reads the original as frozen reuse and never edits it; the worklist
stays in the original tracker, appended with `[<T>M<N>-<id>]`; a bump reaches every live track the
same day.

**OPT-F · Sibling research tracks** — peers over shared code and data: one directory per track
with its own instruction files (identical), tracker and `milestones/` — the common root carries
none, since some agents also load every folder above the one they start in; one shared
`PLAYBOOK.md` and `tools/pb/` at the common root, the toolkit owned by one track (§6) and called
by each track with explicit flags — `plan.py --plan <track plan>` (or from inside the track's
folder), `verify.py <scope> --manifest tools/pb/tracks/<track>/verify.json --root <track>`;
baselines transcribed from a sibling's recorded run with a path
citation, never re-derived; cross-repo reuse name-resolved, never hard-coded; a dead thesis stays
in place, a dormant track reads `DEFERRED (<why> — wake: <condition>)`; each track clears its own
TL.

**OPT-G · Client engagement** — billing is never an agent's call (out-of-scope work is flagged in
the handoff and tracker; the ruling is the lead's) · identity scrub for resale (the client's name,
logo and marks confined to dev docs, standing law from the renaming milestone) · demo and real
install provably isolated (asked before built) · client data is OPT-D data · a platform-locked
verification carries a twin banner until resolved, with the lead's pre-authorization for its own
removal · client meetings leave a tracker-indexed question doc with answer slots.

---

## §14 Templates

### §14.1 Plan skeleton (`milestones/m<N>/m<N>_implementation_plan.md`)
```markdown
# M<N> — <goal> (size class: S|M|L)
<Goal paragraph.>
Models: <agent> — <model>, <level> (gate) · <model>, <level> (usual) · <model>, <level> · … — strongest first; read <YYYY-MM-DD> from <its model pages | the probe> and the lead's answer
Changes by asking: PLAN only | every block — <one line why>
Launch prompt: « Read milestones/m<N>/m<N>_implementation_plan.md and execute M<N>-<id>
yourself — you are the task agent, not an orchestrator. Grep for your own heading first. »

## Resolve with the lead first
<open design calls — answered at TP by the question tool (§12), ≤4 per call; each restates its consequence>

## Flow
| Task | Type | Goal | Order | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M<N>-TI | PLAN + LEAD answers | <one line> | FIRST | TODO |
| … | … | … | AFTER <id> · ∥ set-X (<why disjoint>) · LAST | … |
<the jump index, one row per block, in EXECUTION order; kept in sync by tools/pb/plan.py>

## Rules
PLAYBOOK.md v<X> + the instruction file bind every agent (cited once, never per block). Full text of every
rule below: m<N>_rules.md. Header budget: <600 / 60 unless tuned>. Rating refresh: <5 flags unless
tuned>. Full-loop budget: <N s on <box>,
measured <date> by <task>>. OPT modules: <list>. Grants (OPT-C lanes: scope · exclusions · ceiling):
<index lines>. Derogations, dated: <index lines>. Sizing bar: §2.1 — ceilings and named exceptions
by id; E blocks: <count>. What a capture cannot show here: <list, or none>.
| # | Rule (one line — the annex carries the text) |
|---|---|

## Superseded / retired (§2.6)
<`<thing> — superseded by <decision> (<date>)`; "none — purely additive" is valid; a pivot plan
that says "none" is a TP finding. Inherited debt (§2.4): <none | id — owner block>.>

## Repo facts (so you don't explore)
<tracker name · stack, entry points, key paths · shells AND machines in play · run commands ·
harness scopes and what each costs · launchers>
### Using the tools — the calls a task needs (never the tool's code or its help)
<one line per call, exactly as it is typed: close your block · file or move a block · test while
you work · the red arm · the last run · the report's own checker — written by TH, kept by every
block that changes a call>
### Hazards (dated, root-caused — carried forward by TZ until retired with a reason)

## Pipeline state (a register of one-line pointers — never a handoff or a history)
- Next task: M<N>-<id>
- Counters: T=<n> · D=<n> · V=<n> · Q=<n> · TI=<n> · TJ=<n> · TV=<n> · TC=<n> · TR=<n> · TM=<n>
- Open D/BLOCKED register: <id — one line — unblocked by | none>
- Outstanding commit gates: <none | list>
- Carryover: <none | m<N>_carryover.md §2, consumed by TZ>
- Awaiting lead: <none | id — the question>
- Model ratings: <YYYY-MM-DD> by <M<N>-<id> | the bootstrap>

# Tasks
<blocks per §3.2, in RUN order, gates first; dynamic blocks placed where they run (E8); closed
blocks stubbed by the V that closes their phase or by TC (§11)>
```

### §14.2 Task block — see §3.2.

### §14.3 Templates (copy, replace <…>; every block's first line after the heading is `- Status:`)
```markdown
## M<N>-D<n> · <short bug title> · **BUILD** · <model>, <level>
- Status: TODO
- Blocks: <M<N>-<id> | nothing>
- Caused by: <M<N>-<id> whose work it repairs, when measured | unknown>
- Files: <paths opened and verified | unverified — the executing task confirms first>
- Read: this file (rules + this task) + <exact files>
- Symptom: <observed + exact repro> · Suspected cause: <hypothesis | unknown>
- Deliver: the smallest fix resolving the symptom without breaking contracts; if the real cause is
  out of scope, stop and ask (§12). One attempt + self-verify; a visual symptom is capture-confirmed.
- Done when: <observable criterion, on <box>>
- Handoff: <placeholder>
```
```markdown
## M<N>-V<n> · Validation — <scope> · **CHECK** · <model>, <level>
- Status: TODO
- Read: contract + handoffs of covered tasks + delivered files, each by the section it covers
- Deliver: contract-vs-code on <scope>; each defect → its own D (E4), files named; every declared
  enforcement re-checked red-armed (`verify.py --redarm`, §8); the verdict names its blind spot,
  its box, and NOT PROVEN where only synthetic or source-only evidence exists. If this V closes a
  phase: stub its DONE blocks (≤10, else append a TC<n>), check the header budget, run the
  complete loop once and record the fast numbers, and run `rung_record.py report` and keep its
  table — per class and rung: blocks, first-try passes on the claim run, cost per DONE block, D
  blocks traced (§2.3) — the record the next ratings read (§0); a class it moves → a `TM<n>`
  filed. A cheaper rating it finds risky with none of §0's rules (1)–(4) to name is an
  observation in its report, never a defect, never a re-rating up (§0, no step up on a guess).
- Done when: verdict recorded; D's created; register updated.
- Handoff: <placeholder>
```
```markdown
## M<N>-TV<n> · UI/UX pass — <phase / screens> · **BUILD** · <model>, <level>
- Status: TODO
- Read: this file (rules + this task) + the capture routes manifest + <how to start the increment>
- Deliver: capture every screen and state <phase> touched (tools/pb/capture_web.mjs or the
  engine's harness) into images/tv<n>/; look at every capture yourself and file a D for each
  thing that does not fit — a wall of text, a janky flow, an overflow, a wrong state (E1, never a
  fix here); build the page and serve it (tools/pb/review_page.py serve) holding only the
  captures that need the lead's eyes, each with your note; wait while the lead pages through —
  remarks land verbatim in reports/review<n>.md. Then ONE triage pass (E10): measure every remark
  against the code, forks to the lead in blocks of ≤4, D blocks filed batched and placed where
  they run; then stop the server (§6). Items a capture cannot show: marked NOT PROVEN (static
  capture).
- Pass: every route captured at every declared viewport; every capture looked at — filed, flagged
  for the lead or passed with a one-line reason; page served; remarks file written; triage filed.
  Fail: a route missing from the set, a capture neither filed, flagged nor passed, or a remark not
  traceable to a capture id.
- Handoff: <placeholder>
```
```markdown
## M<N>-TW · Live walk — the user gate · **LEAD walk + CHECK scribe** · <model>, <level>
- Status: TODO
- Read: this file (rules + this task) + the launchers
- Deliver: the lead drives the increment from the launchers, one step at a time; the scribe
  journals every defect and friction as a numbered line (TW-W1…) in reports/walk<k>.md, no fixes
  mid-walk, and journals its own wrong assertions. Afterwards one triage pass (E10). The scribe may
  MOVE blocks to keep the plan runnable top to bottom (E8, move only).
- Pass: walk completed, journal triaged, register updated. Fail: increment won't boot — a NO-GO
  defect to fix and re-walk. Approval withheld is a NO-GO, never an approval with a punch list.
- Handoff: <placeholder>
```
```markdown
## M<N>-TH · Test harness and toolkit · **BUILD** · <model>, <level> · (AFTER the contract, BEFORE TG)
- Status: TODO
- Read: this file (whole) + the contract + PLAYBOOK.md annex §A
- Deliver: the toolkit extracted from annex §A into tools/pb/ (§B.4's one line, `<dest>` = `.`),
  every tool's selftest GO — a tool the stack cannot run marked `NOT RUN (<missing dependency>)` ·
  the manifests written from Repo facts: verify.json with one scope per contract surface (its
  count parser, expected count, paths, planted bugs, `box:` where one box is needed), launch.json
  and the double-click start/stop files (§A.3: `launch.py start --task <id>`, Enter, `launch.py
  stop`), routes.json when there is UI · `.*implementation_plan.md.lock` in `.gitignore` where
  `milestones/` is tracked (the plan tool's lock file, §A.1) · plan_lint wired as a scope · the
  coverage anchor seeded · docs/agent/testing.md (how to add a scope; cost per scope) · the
  routine (§4 Rule 4) written into Rules · the "Using the tools" part of Repo facts · one measured
  complete loop per box → the budget line.
- Verify: `python3 tools/pb/verify.py --all --task M<N>-TH` · Pass: GO, every scope present, loop
  time recorded · Fail: a scope with zero cases, or a verdict not derived from counts.
- Handoff: <placeholder>
```
The adoption pair for a project already under way, one block each: `<x>` and `<y>` are the next

where there is none (an id is never reused, §3.1).

     Fail: NO-GO — either names another version (`12` is not `12.1`), or none (the Rules' line:
     §14.1's first).
  2. the project's own plan lint — the statuses and the Flow with it
     ~~~bash
     python3 tools/pb/plan.py lint --plan milestones/m<N>/m<N>_implementation_plan.md
     ~~~
     Pass: GO (a build that takes `--plan` first answers a usage error: `plan.py --plan … lint`);
     `NOT RUN (no plan.py before TH-adopt)` where no `tools/pb/plan.py` sits here or up to three
     folders up — the pair brings the annex's.
     Fail: NO-GO — each finding on an open block fixed, the run repeated.
  Drift is the canonical repository's check (§15 (4)), never run here.
- Adversarial: a structural pass that changes a Deliver's meaning or drops a fact — diff every
  moved block byte-for-byte, report the count.
- Handoff: ≤8 lines inline · full record → tasks/M<N>-TE-V<V>.md · Next: <the next block>
```

### §14.4 Contract skeleton (`m<N>_contrat.md`)
```markdown
# M<N> Contract — <scope>   [sections marked FROZEN are immutable]
§0 Conventions & precedence  <prior contracts read-only; this one additive; declared overrides land
   HERE, one line each (§9); arithmetic corrections too; ratified clauses cite the lead's words>
§1 Architecture & registries  §2 Data model  §3 Endpoints  §4 UI (exact product-language strings,
tokens)  §5 Non-regression guarantees (surviving surface only; each names its red-armed mechanism)
§6 Modes / environments (machines in play)  §7 Documentation
```

### §14.5 Launch prompts
- Task: « Read milestones/m<N>/m<N>_implementation_plan.md and execute M<N>-<id> yourself — you
  are the task agent, not an orchestrator. Grep for your own heading first (§4 Rule 1). » In
  Claude Code the switch (§B.3) reads this line, or « do task <id> » with the plan open in the
  editor, and runs a switched block on its rung — nothing typed ahead of it.
- Orchestrator (opt-in): « Read milestones/m<N>/m<N>_implementation_plan.md. You are an
  orchestrator for tasks <list>. Follow PLAYBOOK §7; serve the status page. »
- Bootstrap: « Read PLAYBOOK.md. My project is: <description>. Bootstrap it. »
- Adoption: « Read milestones/m<N>/m<N>_implementation_plan.md and execute M<N>-TE-V<V> yourself. »

## §A Annex — the toolkit, in full source

Nothing ships with this file: the instruction files are generated from §C, and every tool below
is carried here in full source, each file in one marked block — a pb:file marker line naming its
path and md5, then a four-tilde fence holding the file byte for byte. `TH` extracts them at
bootstrap, `TH-adopt` in a project already under way, with §B.4's one line. The source is the
contract: a tool extracted from here behaves the same in every project, so the routine, the lint
and the review page mean the same thing everywhere. A project may fix or extend its copy; the
next bump's census diffs every project's build against this annex and carries the changes worth
keeping — nothing is copied sideways between projects. Each tool's section below says what it
does and the calls a task makes; its marked block follows the section.

### §A.0 Conventions for every tool
- **Python 3.9+, standard library only**, one file per tool in `tools/pb/`, runnable on every box
  in play (Windows and POSIX). Two files are JavaScript: the browser driver (§A.5), on Node and
  the Playwright the project already has, and the switch (§A.10), a Claude Code plugin module that
  imports nothing and needs Node only to run its own commands. A tool that needs a dependency the project does not have is
  not built; the annex names the fallback.
- **Every command ends in the §4 Rule 4 shape** — one counts line, failures only when non-zero,
  `=== GO ===` / `=== NO-GO: <reason> ===` derived from counts, exit 0 / 1, `--json` behind a flag.
- **`selftest` on every tool, red-armed:** it builds a throwaway fixture, proves the tool goes
  GO on a clean fixture, plants one violation and proves it goes NO-GO, and cleans up only what it
  created. A tool without a red-armed selftest is not done (§8).
- **Files:** read and write UTF-8, and print UTF-8 safely on any console; detect and preserve the
  edited file's line endings, and hash CRLF-stripped bytes (a CRLF checkout is not a change);
  write atomically (temp file, then replace), retrying the replace while another process still
  holds the file open; take a lock around every read-modify-write of the plan; never delete a
  file; never run a git write on the project — a selftest's own temp repository is its fixture
  (content_gate.py's `git init` / `git add`); read-only git (`rev-parse`, `status --porcelain`)
  is allowed for the tree hash, always with `--no-optional-locks`, so a read never writes the
  index.
- **Logs are keyed by task:** every command that writes a log takes `--task <ID>` (or `$PB_TASK`)
  and refuses to run without one — no shared `adhoc` log.
- **Manifests are JSON in `tools/pb/`**, written by TH from the plan's Repo facts: `verify.json`,
  `launch.json`, `routes.json`, `denylist.txt`. They are the one project-specific part of the
  folder and never enter this annex.
- **Size:** no line count binds an annex tool — it is read once, by the agent who extracts it;
  `plan.py` and `status_page.py` share the plan-parsing functions (`status_page.py` imports from
  `plan.py`).

### §A.1 `plan.py` — every edit an agent makes to the plan, one call each
**What it reads.** A *block* starts at a heading matching
`^## (\d*M[\d.]+[a-z]?-[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9.-]*)` — group 1 the id, a letter-suffixed milestone
(`M1b-`) included — and runs to the line before the next `^## ` or `^# ` heading; a heading inside
a code fence is no block. Its *trailer* is not the block's: from the first section line under its
last field — a `###`–`######` heading, or a `>` line or `---` rule at column 0 after a blank line: a
phase's `> **Commit gate (lead):**` banner (§10), the next lot's heading, a closing note — to the
block's end; with none, the `>` lines and `---` rules at its end, blanks between. Every write leaves
the trailer where it sits, and no field runs over a quote or a rule after a blank; a `>` line glued
to the body (no blank above it) is the body's, and a `###` with a field below it is the block's own.
The *header* is everything above the first block; its sections are
`## Flow`, `## Rules`, `## Superseded`, `## Repo facts`, `## Pipeline state` (prefix match). A
block's *Status line* is its first non-blank line; its *type* is the bold tag after its title,
read against §0's id table (`BUILDER`, `JUDGE`, `HUMAN` read as their aliases); its *rating* is
the segment right after that tag, `<model>, <level>` or a role word (§3.2) — the level the text
after the last `, `, so a model with a dot, a hyphen or a comma parses whole — and a ` · switch`
right after it is read, never written; an order clause, `∥ set-X` or an old stub's `**DONE (…)**`
is never a rating. The *ladder* is the header's `Models:` line once it reads `<agent> — <rung> ·
… — strongest first; …` (§0); any other `Models:` line is no ladder. In a plan with a ladder, the
*refresh count* is the carried flags on BUILD blocks still to run — by §0's id table or a `Kind
kept:` line, a `LEAD go` tag left out — whose `[<from>, <date>]` stamp, read through bold and a
backticked id, is later than the register's `Model ratings: <date> by <id>` line (all of them
when it has none), an unstamped flag counted only while it has none; its threshold is the Rules'
`Rating refresh: <n> flags`, default 5 (§0). The plan is
`--plan <path>`, else the newest `milestones/*/*implementation_plan.md` holding a TODO or
IN PROGRESS block.
**The calls** (the ones a task makes also sit in the plan's Repo facts, §14.1):
- `show <ID>` — the block's start read: the header without its Flow table, then the block (§4
  Rule 1; a v12 trial).
- `status <ID> <STATUS…>` — the Status line and its Flow cell (a per-task table's) in one write;
  stamps the date and time on `IN PROGRESS` and `DONE`, the start time beside `DONE`; refuses a
  state outside §3.3.
- `close <ID> --status "<STATUS>" (--text "<t>" | --file <md>) [--set "<Key>=<value>"]…` —
  status, handoff and register lines in one write: a task's whole close-out.
- `handoff <ID> --text "<t>" | --file <md>` — replaces the `- Handoff:` field; NO-GO above 8 lines
  unless `--allow-long`.
- `flag <ID> "<text>" --from <ID>` — appends `· [<from>, <date>] <text>` to `- Carried flags:`,
  creating the field under the Status line when absent; in a plan with a ladder it prints the
  refresh count against the threshold (`refresh=<n>/<t>`), and a WARN carrying the `append` call
  of a `TM<n>` once the count reaches it with no rating pass pending.
- `append <block.md> --after <ID> [--goal "<one line>"]` — inserts the block after the anchor's
  body, above its trailer (a WARN names it), writes its type from §0's table when the heading has
  none — before the rating — adds its Flow row (none where the Flow is read as history, below)
  and increments its counter (`TM=<n>` for a rating pass, PLAN). Once the header carries a
  ladder it refuses a block, Q aside, whose heading has no rating or one off the ladder, and
  prints the ladder's rungs; it never writes a rating or a switch.
- `move <ID> --after <ID2>` — moves the block and its Flow row, its trailer left where it sat, to
  above the anchor's trailer (a WARN names each); refuses if a byte would change.
- `stub <ID>… [--archive <file>] [--by <ID>]` — DONE / N/A / DEFERRED blocks only: the full block
  to the archive, verified byte for byte, the stub left in place with the block's own date kept
  and its trailer under it (a WARN names it).
- `register --set "<Key>=<value>"…` — rewrites `- <Key>:` lines in the Pipeline state; refuses a
  malformed key; a new key is appended at the section's end.
- `lint [--budget-header 600] [--budget-pipeline 60] [--tc-margin 100]` — v12's rules bind the blocks still to run
  (TODO · IN PROGRESS · BLOCKED · DEFERRED, and a status that names no state). Red on such a
  block: a first line that is not `- Status:` · a status outside §3.3 · a heading not preceded by
  a blank line · a `Verify:` with a command and no `Pass:`/`Fail:` · a type that disagrees with
  the id, unless a `Kind kept:` line in Repo facts names it (§0) · in a per-task Flow table, no
  row, or a row off its state or date · once the header carries a ladder, a heading (Q aside)
  with no rating, a role word or a rating off the ladder (` · switch` never required). Red on the
  plan: a Flow row naming an id with no block · a duplicate id or Flow row · a run tag inside a
  fence · header or Pipeline state over its budget by more than the margin (§11) with no TODO
  `TC<n>` · a ladder whose rung does not
  parse or that marks other than one `(gate)` and one `(usual)` · the refresh count at its
  threshold with no `TM<n>` TODO or IN PROGRESS — the message the `append` call that files one,
  after a TODO `TC<n>`, else after the block in progress, else before the first TODO (§0, §11) ·
  `logs/ reports/ tasks/ images/` missing. A plan with no ladder — every plan before its v12.1 adoption — is never red for a
  rating: one WARN counts its unrated open blocks. A finished block is never rated after the
  fact, nor counted. Read as history — counted on one
  `HISTORY:` line, never a failure: a finished block (DONE · N/A, read by the first state word of
  its Status — bold, notes and the plan's own language allowed — else of a `- Status:` further
  down or of an old stub's heading) · a Flow that is prose, a phase table or absent (no block
  needs a row; `append` and `status` write none) · a gate row in a per-task table (it names no
  block). Warns on: a `Read:` naming a file over ~500 lines whole · a line repeated in three or
  more blocks · a `Deliver:` item no Verify step names · a DONE block outside the current phase
  longer than a stub · a handoff over 8 lines · a carried flag with no stamp on an open BUILD
  block (named: counted before the first `Model ratings:` line, history after it).
- `header` — lines per header section and the total, against the budgets; with a ladder, the
  refresh count against its threshold too.
- `selftest` — every call round-tripped on a synthetic plan; each lint rule planted and shown red.
Every call prints one counts line and `=== GO ===` / `=== NO-GO: <reason> ===`; a wrong call prints
the right one. Every write call locks the plan through `.<plan file>.lock` beside it, a file left
in place and never deleted (§A.0); a read (`show`, `lint`, `header`) leaves none.

#### `tools/pb/plan.py`
<!-- pb:file tools/pb/plan.py md5:4886df610160 -->
~~~~python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""tools/pb/plan.py - every edit an agent makes to the plan, one call each.

PLAYBOOK annex §A.0 (conventions) + §A.1 (this tool).  Python 3.9+, standard library only.
UTF-8 in and out, on any console; line endings preserved; atomic writes, the replace retried
while another process still holds the file; a lock around every read-modify-write; nothing
deleted; git never run.  Every call ends in the §4 Rule 4 shape: one counts line,
failures/warnings only when non-zero, `=== GO ===` / `=== NO-GO: <reason> ===` derived from the
counts, exit 0 / 1, `--json` behind a flag.  A refusal writes nothing and says so; a wrong call
prints the right one.

The calls: show · status · close · handoff · flag · append · move · stub · register · lint ·
header · selftest.  The ones a task makes sit in the plan's Repo facts (PLAYBOOK §14.1).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import contextlib
import datetime
import glob
import io
import json
import os
import re
import shutil
import stat
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time

try:                      # POSIX advisory locks
    import fcntl
except ImportError:       # pragma: no cover - Windows has none
    fcntl = None
try:                      # Windows byte-range locks
    import msvcrt
except ImportError:       # pragma: no cover - POSIX has none
    msvcrt = None

PROG = "python3 tools/pb/plan.py"
STATES = ("N/A", "IN PROGRESS", "TODO", "DONE", "BLOCKED", "DEFERRED")
STUBBABLE = ("DONE", "N/A", "DEFERRED")
OPEN_STATES = ("TODO", "IN PROGRESS", "BLOCKED", "DEFERRED")
FINISHED = ("DONE", "N/A")      # a finished block is history to the lint: read, counted, never a failure
# A state word in the plan's own language (the fleet's other one is French): read, never written -
# `status` and `append` take §3.3's words only.
LANG_STATES = ((r"[ÀA] FAIRE", "TODO"), (r"EN COURS", "IN PROGRESS"),
               (r"TERMIN[ÉE]E?S?|FAITE?S?|COMPL[ÉE]T[ÉE]E?S?", "DONE"), (r"BLOQU[ÉE]E?S?", "BLOCKED"),
               (r"REPORT[ÉE]E?S?|DIFF[ÉE]R[ÉE]E?S?", "DEFERRED"), (r"ANNUL[ÉE]E?S?|SANS OBJET", "N/A"))
# A block heading: group 1 the id, a letter-suffixed milestone (`M1b-`) included (§A.1).
BLOCK_RE = re.compile(r"^## (\d*M[\d.]+[a-z]?-[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9.-]*)")
ID_START_RE = re.compile(r"[\[\s]*\d*M[\d.]+[a-z]?-[A-Za-z]")   # a Flow cell naming a block id
STATUS_FIELD_RE = re.compile(r"^- \**Status\**:")
# `Kind kept: <id> = <TYPE> (<the kind its plan gave it>)` - a Repo facts line, one per pre-v12 id (§0).
KIND_KEPT_RE = re.compile(r"Kind kept:\**\s*`?(\d*M[\d.]+[a-z]?-[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9.-]*?)`?\s*=\s*\**"
                          r"(BUILD|CHECK|PLAN|MOVE)(?![A-Za-z])")
TOP_RE = re.compile(r"^##? ")
TRAILER_RE = re.compile(r">|[ \t]*-{3,}[ \t]*$")   # a trailer line: a `>` banner line, a `---` rule
SUBHEAD_RE = re.compile(r"#{3,6}(?:[ \t]|$)")      # a `###`-`######` heading at column 0: a lot, a phase's part
GATE_RE = re.compile(r">|-{3,}[ \t]*$")            # at column 0: a `>` gate line, a `---` rule
HEADER_SECTIONS = ("## Flow", "## Rules", "## Superseded", "## Repo facts", "## Pipeline state")
FIELD_RE = re.compile(r"^- ([A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9 ()/'-]*):")
DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}")
OPEN_RE = re.compile(r"^[ \t]*(?:[-*+]|\d+[.)])?[ \t]*(`{3,}|~{3,})")
RUNTAG_RE = re.compile(r"\[(ALREADY RUN|NOT RUN)")
PASS_RE, FAIL_RE = re.compile(r"Pass(\*\*)?:"), re.compile(r"Fail(\*\*)?:")
TAG_RE = re.compile(r"\*\*([^*]+)\*\*")
STAMP_RE = re.compile(r"IN PROGRESS \((\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}) (\d{2}:\d{2})\)")
TODAY = datetime.date.today().isoformat()
DIRS = ("logs", "reports", "tasks", "images")
READ_CAP = 500            # a `Read:` file over this many lines is named by section or range (§11)
REPEAT_MIN = 50           # shorter lines repeat by nature (field labels, placeholders)

# §3.3's closed set: a status starts with one of these words and has that word's shape.
CLOSED_SET = (
    ("TODO", re.compile(r"TODO$"),
     "TODO stands alone - a stall is BLOCKED (+ what unblocks it) or IN PROGRESS (awaiting: <question>)"),
    ("IN PROGRESS", re.compile(r"IN PROGRESS(?: \(.+)?$"),
     "IN PROGRESS stands alone or carries its (<stamp>) / (awaiting: <question>)"),
    ("DONE", re.compile(r"DONE\b.*\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}"), "DONE carries its date: DONE (YYYY-MM-DD ...)"),
    ("BLOCKED", re.compile(r"BLOCKED(?![A-Za-z])"), ""),
    ("DEFERRED", re.compile(r"DEFERRED \(wake: .+"),
     "DEFERRED carries its trigger: DEFERRED (wake: <condition>)"),
    ("N/A", re.compile(r"N/A(?![A-Za-z])"), ""),
)
CLOSED_SHAPES = ("TODO · IN PROGRESS (<stamp> | awaiting: <question>) · DONE (<date> ...) · BLOCKED "
                 "(+ what unblocks it) | BLOCKED-D<n> · DEFERRED (wake: <condition>) · N/A (<reason>)")

# §0's block types, set by the id.  Pre-v12 words (BUILDER, JUDGE, HUMAN and their composites)
# are read as aliases: never flagged, never rewritten.  A pre-v12 id whose plan gave it another
# kind carries it in a `Kind kept:` Repo facts line, which the lint reads before this table.
TYPES = ("BUILD", "CHECK", "PLAN", "MOVE")
ID_TYPES = (              # (the id's local part, the types it may carry, the tag `append` writes)
    (r"TC\d", ("MOVE",), "MOVE"),
    (r"TC(?![A-Za-z0-9])", ("PLAN",), "PLAN"),
    (r"TE-V\d", ("PLAN", "MOVE"), "PLAN"),               # MOVE: a TE-V<N>'s rename sweep
    (r"TB-V", ("PLAN",), "PLAN"),
    (r"TH(?![A-Z])", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),
    (r"TW(?![A-Z])", ("CHECK",), "LEAD walk + CHECK"),
    (r"TV", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),
    (r"TR", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),
    (r"TD\d*(?:[a-z]\d*)?(?![A-Za-z0-9])", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),         # TD, TDa - never TDOC
    (r"TE(?!-V\d)\d*(?:[a-z]\d*)?(?![A-Za-z0-9])", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),  # TE, TE2, TEa, TE-<name>
    (r"T[LA]", ("CHECK",), "CHECK"),
    (r"T[IJ]", ("PLAN",), "PLAN + LEAD answers"),
    (r"T[PGBZ]", ("PLAN",), "PLAN"),
    (r"TM\d", ("PLAN",), "PLAN"),                         # the rating pass (v12.1); a bare v9 `TM` is no id here
    (r"DBG", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),
    (r"[TRD]\d", ("BUILD",), "BUILD"),
    (r"V(?:\d|[a-z](?![A-Za-z]))", ("CHECK",), "CHECK"),                 # V1, and a split `Vf`
    (r"Q\d", (), "LEAD answers"),
)

# The calls, exactly as typed: a wrong call prints its line (§A.1).
CALLS = {
    "show": "show <ID>",
    "status": 'status <ID> "<STATUS>"',
    "close": 'close <ID> --status "<STATUS>" --text "<handoff>" [--set "<Key>=<value>"]...',
    "handoff": 'handoff <ID> --text "<handoff>"   (or --file <md>)',
    "flag": 'flag <ID> "<text>" --from <ID>',
    "append": 'append <block.md> --after <ID> [--goal "<one line>"]',
    "move": "move <ID> --after <ID2>",
    "stub": "stub <ID>... --by <ID> [--archive <file>]",
    "register": 'register --set "<Key>=<value>"...',
    "lint": "lint [--budget-header 600] [--budget-pipeline 60] [--tc-margin 100]",
    "header": "header [--budget-header 600] [--budget-pipeline 60]",
    "selftest": "selftest",
}
REMOVED = {"dt": "`dt` left with the D:T count (v12 §2.3: D per task is read from git by the audits)"}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- files
def read_text(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
        raw = fh.read()
    text = raw.decode("utf-8")
    nl = "\r\n" if "\r\n" in text else "\n"
    return text.replace("\r\n", "\n"), nl


class PlanWriteError(OSError):
    """The atomic write could not land.  Reported as a counted `REFUSED:` line, never as
    a traceback: a NO-GO writes nothing *and* says so."""


REPLACE_ATTEMPTS = 6          # 0.05+0.1+0.2+0.4+0.8 s of backoff, then refuse


def _injected_replace_failure():
    """Test seams: `PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_SKIP=<k>` lets the next k replaces through, then
    `PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_FAIL=<n>` fails the next n.

    A replace refused on Windows is a race against a scanner or an indexer holding the file,
    so it cannot be *scheduled*; the seam forces it, which is how the retry arm and the
    archive roll-back are red-armed on any box.
    """
    skip = int(os.environ.get("PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_SKIP", "0") or 0)
    if skip > 0:
        os.environ["PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_SKIP"] = str(skip - 1)
        return False
    n = int(os.environ.get("PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_FAIL", "0") or 0)
    if n <= 0:
        return False
    os.environ["PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_FAIL"] = str(n - 1)
    return True


def backoff():
    """The first retry's wait, doubling after; `PB_PLAN_TEST_BACKOFF` shortens it for the selftest."""
    return float(os.environ.get("PB_PLAN_TEST_BACKOFF", "0.05") or 0.05)


def replace_atomic(tmp, path, attempts=REPLACE_ATTEMPTS):
    """os.replace, retried on a transient PermissionError.

    On Windows a scanner or the search indexer can still hold the just-closed temp file (or
    the destination) for a few hundred ms; os.replace then raises PermissionError
    [WinError 5] - measured in the fleet twice in 8 selftest runs, once turning a full loop
    red.  The write stays atomic either way (the destination is never partial), so a bounded
    retry is safe; a permanent failure becomes a PlanWriteError that main() turns into a
    `REFUSED:` line.
    """
    delay, last = backoff(), None
    for i in range(attempts):
        try:
            if _injected_replace_failure():
                raise PermissionError(5, "injected [WinError 5] (PB_PLAN_TEST_REPLACE_FAIL)")
            os.replace(tmp, path)
            if i:
                print("WARN: replace of %s needed %d attempts - transient lock on this box"
                      % (os.path.basename(path), i + 1))
            return
        except PermissionError as exc:
            last = exc
            if i < attempts - 1:
                time.sleep(delay)
                delay *= 2
    raise PlanWriteError("could not replace %s after %d attempts (%s) - nothing was written"
                         % (path, attempts, last))


def _drop(tmp):
    """Remove a temp that never landed, retried like the replace: on Windows the process that
    refused the replace may still hold the temp too.  A temp that stays is left, never raised."""
    delay = backoff()
    for i in range(REPLACE_ATTEMPTS):
        try:
            os.unlink(tmp)
            return
        except FileNotFoundError:
            return
        except PermissionError:
            if i == REPLACE_ATTEMPTS - 1:
                return
            time.sleep(delay)
            delay *= 2


def write_text(path, text, nl):
    data = text.replace("\n", nl).encode("utf-8")
    folder = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(path)) or "."
    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=folder, prefix=".pbplan.", suffix=".tmp")
    try:
        with os.fdopen(fd, "wb") as fh:
            fh.write(data)
        if os.path.exists(path):          # mkstemp makes the temp 0600: keep the file's own mode
            shutil.copymode(path, tmp)
        replace_atomic(tmp, path)
    finally:
        if os.path.exists(tmp):
            _drop(tmp)


LOCK_TIMEOUT_S = float(os.environ.get("PB_PLAN_LOCK_TIMEOUT", "20") or 20)
WRITE_CMDS = ("status", "close", "handoff", "flag", "append", "move", "stub", "register")


def lock_path_for(path):
    """A SIBLING lock file, never the plan itself: write_text lands by os.replace, and on
    Windows an open handle to the destination blocks that replace."""
    folder = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(path)) or "."
    return os.path.join(folder, "." + os.path.basename(path) + ".lock")


class PlanLock:
    """Advisory lock held across the whole load -> modify -> save window (§A.0).

    Every write call is a read-modify-write.  Unlocked, two agents that load before either
    saves both print `=== GO ===` with exit 0 and the second silently overwrites the first -
    measured in the fleet: 2 of 4 and 3 of 4 concurrent `status` writes landed, all four
    green.  A lost `- Handoff:` is a lost report.

    fcntl.flock on POSIX, msvcrt.locking on Windows; on any third platform this degrades to a
    documented no-op with a printed WARN rather than an ImportError.  On timeout the caller
    REFUSES - it never falls through to an unprotected write.
    """

    def __init__(self, path, timeout=None, shared=False):
        self.path = lock_path_for(path)
        self.timeout = LOCK_TIMEOUT_S if timeout is None else timeout
        self.shared = shared
        self.fh = None
        self.noop = fcntl is None and msvcrt is None

    def _try_once(self):
        if fcntl is not None:
            fcntl.flock(self.fh.fileno(), (fcntl.LOCK_SH if self.shared else fcntl.LOCK_EX)
                        | fcntl.LOCK_NB)
        else:
            self.fh.seek(0)
            msvcrt.locking(self.fh.fileno(), msvcrt.LK_NBLCK, 1)

    def acquire(self):
        if self.noop:
            print("WARN: no advisory file locking on this platform (%s) - concurrent"
                  " plan.py writes are UNPROTECTED" % sys.platform)
            return True
        self.fh = open(self.path, "a+b")
        deadline = time.time() + self.timeout
        while True:
            try:
                self._try_once()
                return True
            except OSError:
                if time.time() >= deadline:
                    self.fh.close()
                    self.fh = None
                    return False
                time.sleep(0.05)

    def release(self):
        if self.fh is None:
            return
        try:
            if fcntl is not None:
                fcntl.flock(self.fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_UN)
            else:
                self.fh.seek(0)
                msvcrt.locking(self.fh.fileno(), msvcrt.LK_UNLCK, 1)
        except OSError:
            pass
        self.fh.close()
        self.fh = None

    def __enter__(self):
        return self.acquire()

    def __exit__(self, *exc):
        self.release()
        return False


def load_delay():
    """Test seam: `PB_PLAN_TEST_DELAY=<s>` widens load -> save so the race is FORCED rather
    than hoped for: a concurrency case must not pass merely because the box was fast."""
    d = float(os.environ.get("PB_PLAN_TEST_DELAY", "0") or 0)
    if d > 0:
        time.sleep(d)


def clock():
    """HH:MM for the §3.3 stamps; `PB_PLAN_TEST_CLOCK` pins it for the selftest."""
    return os.environ.get("PB_PLAN_TEST_CLOCK") or datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%H:%M")


def default_plan():
    cands = sorted(glob.glob("milestones/*/*implementation_plan.md"), key=os.path.getmtime, reverse=True)
    for path in cands:
        text, _ = read_text(path)
        for ln in text.split("\n"):
            if ln.startswith("- Status:") and state_of(ln.split(":", 1)[1]) in ("TODO", "IN PROGRESS"):
                return path
    return cands[0] if cands else None


# ------------------------------------------------------------------------- parsing
def fence_spans(lines):
    """[(open, end)] of every code fence, `end` exclusive: a heading inside one is no block.

    A fence opens on three or more backticks or tildes, after any indentation and at most one
    list marker (`  1. ` then the fence, as Verify steps are written); it closes on a line of
    the same character, as many or more, alone on its line (blanks and a CR trimmed).  A fence
    opened off column 0 also ends at the first non-blank line that starts at column 0 - its
    list item ends there - and that line is read as ordinary text.  A plain toggle on any
    fence-looking line loses real blocks on plans that open fences after a list marker
    (measured in the fleet: 1 block for 80, 11 for 24, 6 for 26).
    """
    spans, i, n = [], 0, len(lines)
    while i < n:
        m = OPEN_RE.match(lines[i])
        if not m:
            i += 1
            continue
        ch, width = m.group(1)[0], len(m.group(1))
        indented = lines[i][:1] not in ("`", "~")
        j, end = i + 1, None
        while j < n:
            t = lines[j].strip(" \t\r")
            if t[:1] == ch and len(t) >= width and t == ch * len(t):
                end = j + 1
                break
            if indented and re.match(r"[^ \t\r]", lines[j]):
                end = j
                break
            j += 1
        end = n if end is None else end
        spans.append((i, end))
        i = end
    return spans


def fence_mask(lines, spans=None):
    mask = [False] * len(lines)
    for lo, hi in (fence_spans(lines) if spans is None else spans):
        for k in range(lo, hi):
            mask[k] = True
    return mask


def gate_line(lines, mask, i):
    """A `>` line or a `---` rule at column 0, outside a fence, with a blank line above it: where a
    phase's gate starts (§10).  Glued to the line above, a `>` line is that line's quote."""
    return not mask[i] and GATE_RE.match(lines[i]) is not None and i > 0 and not lines[i - 1].strip()


def body_end(lines, mask, start, end):
    """One past a block's last body line.  The block's *trailer* is not the block's: from the first
    section line under its last field - a `###`-`######` heading, or a gate line (`gate_line`): a
    phase's `> **Commit gate (lead):**` banner (§10), the next lot's heading, a closing note after
    them - to the span's end; with none, the `>` lines and `---` rules at the span's end, blanks
    between.  A `>` line glued to the body (no blank above it) is the body's: a quote that ends a
    field; a `###` with a field of the block below it is the block's own."""
    fields = [i for i in range(start + 1, end) if not mask[i] and FIELD_RE.match(lines[i])]
    for i in range(fields[-1] + 1 if fields else end, end):
        if not mask[i] and (SUBHEAD_RE.match(lines[i]) or gate_line(lines, mask, i)):
            while i > start + 1 and not mask[i - 1] and not lines[i - 1].strip():
                i -= 1
            return i
    k = end
    while k > start + 1 and not mask[k - 1] and (not lines[k - 1].strip() or TRAILER_RE.match(lines[k - 1])):
        k -= 1
    if lines[k - 1].strip():
        while k < end and lines[k].startswith(">"):
            k += 1
    return k


class Block:
    def __init__(self, bid, start, end, lines, mask):
        self.id, self.start, self.end, self._lines, self._mask = bid, start, end, lines, mask
        self.last = body_end(lines, mask, start, end)

    @property
    def lines(self):
        return self._lines[self.start:self.end]

    @property
    def body(self):
        """The block's own lines, heading first: its trailer and the blanks around it left out."""
        return self._lines[self.start:self.last]

    @property
    def trailer(self):
        """The trailer's lines, blanks dropped: [] for a block that has none."""
        return [l for l in self._lines[self.last:self.end] if l.strip()]

    @property
    def heading(self):
        return self._lines[self.start]

    def status_index(self):
        for i in range(self.start + 1, self.end):
            if self._lines[i].strip():
                return i
        return -1

    @property
    def status_line(self):
        i = self.status_index()
        return self._lines[i] if i >= 0 else ""

    @property
    def status_text(self):
        ln = self.status_line
        return ln.split(":", 1)[1].strip() if ln.startswith("- Status:") else None

    @property
    def state(self):
        ln = self.status_line
        return state_of(ln.split(":", 1)[1]) if ":" in ln else None

    def field(self, name):
        """(first_line_index, end_index_exclusive) of `- <name>:` incl. continuations,
        trailing blank lines excluded so an edit never eats a block separator, nor the trailer; a
        line inside a fence neither starts nor ends a field, and no field runs over a gate line
        (a quote or a rule after a blank: a note between two fields is no field's)."""
        want = "- %s:" % name
        for i in range(self.start + 1, self.last):
            if not self._mask[i] and self._lines[i].startswith(want):
                j = i + 1
                while j < self.last and (self._mask[j] or not (FIELD_RE.match(self._lines[j])
                                                              or self._lines[j].startswith("## ")
                                                              or gate_line(self._lines, self._mask, j))):
                    j += 1
                while j - 1 > i and not self._lines[j - 1].strip():
                    j -= 1
                return i, j
        return None

    def field_text(self, name):
        span = self.field(name)
        if not span:
            return None
        return "\n".join(self._lines[span[0]:span[1]]).split(":", 1)[1]


def state_of(text):
    """A §3.3 state, anchored: the status text *starts* with it, as a whole word, after bold or
    code marks. `ALMOST DONE` is not a state; a state word in the plan's own language (`TERMINÉ`,
    `À FAIRE`) reads as its state, and nothing else does."""
    t = text.strip().lstrip("*`").strip().upper()
    for st in STATES:
        if re.match(re.escape(st) + r"(?!\w)", t):
            return st
    for rx, st in LANG_STATES:
        if re.match(r"(?:%s)(?!\w)" % rx, t):
            return st
    return None


def block_state(b):
    """The state the lint reads a block by, wherever an old plan keeps it: the `- Status:` first
    line, else the first `- Status:` field further down, else the heading after its type tag (an
    old stub's `**JUDGE** · **DONE (2026-08-12)**`); None when none of them starts with a state."""
    if b.status_line.startswith("- Status:"):
        return state_of(b.status_text)
    for i in range(b.start + 1, b.end):
        if not b._mask[i] and STATUS_FIELD_RE.match(b._lines[i]):
            return state_of(b._lines[i].split(":", 1)[1])
    tag = TAG_RE.search(b.heading)
    for seg in re.split(r"\s[·—–]\s", b.heading[tag.end():]) if tag else ():
        st = state_of(re.sub(r"^[\s(*`_]*(?:Status\W*)?", "", seg))
        if st:
            return st
    return None


def status_error(text):
    """Why `text` is off §3.3's closed set, else None: a stall written in prose or in another
    language is refused, never guessed, so it is counted like any other status."""
    t = text.strip()
    for st, shape, why in CLOSED_SET:
        if re.match(re.escape(st) + r"(?![A-Za-z])", t):
            return None if shape.match(t) else "%r: %s" % (t, why)
    return "%r is off §3.3's closed set - the statuses: %s" % (t, CLOSED_SHAPES)


def stamped(text, old, date, now):
    """§3.3's stamps: a bare `IN PROGRESS` / `DONE` (or one carrying only today's date) gains
    the date and time; DONE gains the start time read from the IN PROGRESS stamp it replaces.
    Any other text is written as typed."""
    for st in ("IN PROGRESS", "DONE"):
        if text in (st, "%s (%s)" % (st, date)):
            if st == "IN PROGRESS":
                return "IN PROGRESS (%s %s)" % (date, now)
            m = STAMP_RE.match(old or "")
            if not m:
                return "DONE (%s %s)" % (date, now)
            began = m.group(2) if m.group(1) == date else "%s %s" % (m.group(1), m.group(2))
            return "DONE (%s %s, started %s)" % (date, now, began)
    return text


def id_tail(bid):
    """`M4-TH-adopt-a` -> `TH-adopt-a`; `M12.1-T2a` -> `T2a`."""
    return bid.split("-", 1)[1] if "-" in bid else bid


def id_type(bid):
    """(the types the id may carry, the tag `append` writes) from §0's table, else None."""
    tail = id_tail(bid)
    for rx, allowed, written in ID_TYPES:
        if re.match(rx, tail):
            return allowed, written
    return None


def split_of(tail, tails):
    """True when `tail` is a split of a number the plan already holds (`T24a` beside `T24`,
    `D6b2` beside `D6b`, `TH-d` beside `TH-a`): §3.1 - a split mints no new number."""
    m = re.match(r"^([A-Z]+)(\d*)(.*)$", tail)
    if not m or not m.group(3):
        return False
    cls, num, rest = m.groups()
    if num and rest[:1].islower():
        fam = re.compile(r"^%s%s(?!\d)" % (cls, num))
    elif not num and re.fullmatch(r"(?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+)*-[a-z]", rest):
        fam = re.compile(r"^%s%s-" % (cls, re.escape(rest.rsplit("-", 1)[0])))
    else:
        return False
    return any(t != tail and fam.match(t) for t in tails)


def find_blocks(lines, mask):
    tops = [i for i, ln in enumerate(lines) if not mask[i] and TOP_RE.match(ln)]
    out = []
    for k, i in enumerate(tops):
        m = BLOCK_RE.match(lines[i])
        if m:
            out.append(Block(m.group(1), i, tops[k + 1] if k + 1 < len(tops) else len(lines), lines, mask))
    return out


def header_end(lines, blocks):
    return blocks[0].start if blocks else len(lines)
