  the next milestone"* · *"and less context for agents"* · *"from now on I want simple words.
  Simple explanations. Prioritize bullet points."* · *"Write in the global adopt task to ask
  me if other agents are running or not, wait for my go before applying the v12 edits."* ·
  *"projects can modify their tooling if they find a bug or they need something else. It will
  be picked up by the TZ task when we need to generate V13"* — 2026-09-25: *"ill use the
  agents I want or tell the orchestrators which one I require."* — 2026-09-26: *"can you write
  in the repo facts a small part about how to use the tools instead of agents always looking
  at the code or doing -h"* · *"I would like the playbook to be as most
  agnostic to any type of AI as possible."* · *"Id like to look for better names for the clerk,
  builder and judge types... something that reflects more what they are allowed to do."* · *"If
  the research found something better then I want to go with what the research found."* · *"make
  sure the first agent running the plan tells the user that this plan and its tasks are better
  used in an agent context with coding tools and in automatic mode."* · *"Maybe we can reserve the
  first part of the playbook file to explain to users how to use it. Like they need to clear a use
  a different agent for each task."* · *"Do not re-fire a TR task unless tests take more than
  5s"* — 2026-09-28: *"yes, agents can edit the tracker, its supposed to be obvious."* The comments
  interview: sixteen questions, each answered with the recommended option.
  **Any agent (§B, §C, §1, §12, §15):** the instruction file is `AGENTS.md`, holding §C; `CLAUDE.md`
  and `GEMINI.md` hold the one line `@AGENTS.md`; the rest of the text uses neutral words, and one
  table in §B.3, read once, says where agents differ — the question tool, the automatic mode that
  still asks, sub-agents, memory, the session process. §B opens with a guide for the people
  running the playbook, and the bootstrap closes by naming the automatic mode to use. Measured
  basis: 6 of the 8 agents checked read `AGENTS.md` natively; 31 v11 lines named one agent or its
  tools.
  **Block types (§0, §3, §5, §14):** BUILD · CHECK · PLAN · MOVE, set by the id, plus a LEAD tag
  (go · walk · answers); a checker never fixes, a builder never edits the check that grades it, a
  planner writes no code, a mover rewords nothing; the lint checks the tag and the loop flags a
  write outside the type; the model is the lead's pick, never set by the type; `Changes by
  asking:` replaces the role mode; plans written before v12 keep BUILDER / JUDGE / HUMAN, read by
  id. Research basis: a checker apart from the builder, write scope as the permission, and machine
  checks over prose are the measured findings; no study measures names.
  **Testing (§4 Rule 4, §8, annex §A.2):** a task runs `verify.py --changed` — the scopes its
  changed paths touch, over a coverage anchor where a never-checked path is NO-GO — and the
  complete loop runs at phase-closing V and TR blocks; each scope declares its planted bugs and
  `--redarm` proves them red; every loop record names its box, and a box-bound scope reads
  `owed` elsewhere; TR fires on the loop budget or the lead's word. Measured basis: one project's
  loop 202.3 s → 26.1 s; 82 D from checks that could not fail; 16 D spent building the anchor five
  times over.
  **Reading (§4 Rule 1, §2.3, §11):** `plan.py show` serves a task its start read — the header
  without the Flow table, then its block; a file over ~500 lines is named by section; a line
  repeated in three blocks is stated once; an over-budget header's compaction runs next, ahead of
  any adoption chain, and a bump inserts it first. Measured basis: a task reads 1,690 lines at the
  median before it starts, half of it plan header; plans that adopted v11's budget stay under it.
  **The bug score dropped (§2.3, §2.5, §5, §14):** no D:T lines, allowance, stop trigger, `plan.py
  dt` or `Created by:` lines; the bump's audit counts D per task from the records. Measured basis:
  the stop fired 11 times in the window and was waived every time.
  **UI/UX (§2.3, §8, §14.3, annex §A.4):** a `TV` pass in each phase that adds or changes screens
  — the agent captures and looks at every screen itself, files what does not fit, and the lead's
  page holds only what needs their eyes. Measured basis: 92 UI/UX D found late, in batches, at
  walks.
  **Long runs (§4 Rule 4, §C, OPT-C):** GPU tests and smoke tests are the agent's; a run over 10
  minutes is the lead's, in one visible terminal with a progress bar that shows under `tee`; it
  shares the GPU when its memory fits and resumes from saved state.
  **The lead's time (§2.1, §2.4, §12):** questions and handoffs in plain words; a lead-only step
  still open is asked again at the next gate; an answer given three times becomes a standing one;
  a milestone closes clean, naming any walk never played; TZ splits only when too big.
  **Toolkit (annex §A, §B.4, §1, §14.3):** the annex carries every tool's full source, the best
  build of each chosen on measured results with the other projects' fixes merged; one line
  extracts it; a project's adoption replaces its `plan.py` and `verify.py` and keeps its other
  tools; a project fixes its own copy and the next census harvests the change. Measured basis:
  19 D in 7 projects re-found six tool bug classes.
  **The ceremony (§10, §15):** an adversarial pass first; a dry run of every copy, listed or not,
  with activity signs; one question and one go from the lead; per repository `git pull --ff-only`
  → the writes → `git add -A` → one commit → the push the lead said yes to; the drift check prints
  adoption state apart from copy hashes. A TODO adoption chain is replaced, never stacked.
  **Trials — each kept at the next bump only if its keep test holds:** the served start read (§4
  Rule 1) · sizing a block to its model, and the strong model where the plan is slow (§0) · the
  replay before adopting (§15).
  **Ratified for every project at the bump — a project's `TE-V12` applies these, never asks
  again:** the block types and their aliases · the instruction files · the complete loop per phase
  · the bug score's removal · the plan tool's and the runner's replacement · GPU tests without a
  grant · the TR trigger (the loop budget or the lead's word) · the header's `Models:` and
  `Changes by asking:` lines, a pre-v12 `Role mode:` read as §0 says — these four on the lead's
  answer at the adversarial pass, 2026-09-28: *"Apply, don't re-ask (Recommended)"* · the tracker
  kept current by every agent without asking (§4 Rule 6), on the lead's words above.
  **Loosenings recorded per §15, each on the lead's words — above, or beside the item:** the
  complete loop runs per phase, not per task · the D:T metric, its allowance and its stop trigger
  removed · the `Created by:` field removed · agents run GPU tests without a grant · a TR fires
  only on the loop budget or the lead's word · a mechanical block may run on any model — these
  six asked by name when the lead adopted v12, 2026-09-26:
  *"Adopt, all six included (Recommended)"* · a bump's
  propagation run writes git in each adopter after the lead's go — pull, one commit of everything,
  the push the lead said yes to (§10) — 2026-09-24: *"Everything, like v11"* · *"No, my go is
  enough"* · a TV looks at every capture itself, where v11 kept the image out of a verifier's
  context (§8) — *"Id like for the judge agents doing them to look at the pages themselves …"* ·
  the tracker off Rule 6's ask-first list (§4 Rule 6, §1).
- **v11 — 2026-09-16 — The team playbook.** Renamed from RULEBOOK to PLAYBOOK, rewritten as a
  playbook for a team finishing a project together, compressed, made
  neutral, and extended with a toolkit specified as an annex. Authored from a mining pass over the 20 plans
  that changed since v10, all 23 `CLAUDE.md` files, two tracks bootstrapped under v10 and the
  session transcripts (proposal and evidence kept locally in the canonical repo).
  **Ask (verbatim), the lead, 2026-09-16:** *"I think a good idea to add to the rulebook would
  be for agents to create the harnesses for tests once and agents do all the same testing
  routine. Another idea would be to optimize the tests as most as possible so they take the less
  wall clock time possible to execute."* · *"id also like for V agents to enforce the task
  compression to stubs, and somehow id like for agents to make sure the header of the file is
  compressed from time to time (maybe a dedicated builder job, even if the plan is all judge
  agents). And also id like for the plan to offer a choice between having builder and judge
  agents or only judge agents."* · *"make sure the claude files are identical in all projects so
  the specific rules written by projects in their claude file go into their implementation
  plan"* · *"Id like for the wording to be less bureaucratic and less severe … so it feels more
  like teamwork rather than me invoquing laborers to do hard work … lets rephrase the rulebook so
  its written like a playbook for a team to finish the project toguether."* · *"lets rename it
  everywhere from rulebook.md to playbook.md"* · *"I want agents to prepare the testing
  harnesses and for them to reuse the same ones in the same order and make sure tests are done
  only once when everything is fine. We dont need to loop over the same tests over and over
  again."* · *"make sure theres no mention of any of the projects you scanned today in the
  rulebook. No client name, no [employer] stuff, anything. This is just a perfectly neutral
  multi-tool for ai use"* · *"I felt like the agents placed too many TW walking tasks, there
  shouldnt be as many and they should be automated as much as possible. The agents should gather
  all the images in one html page they boot up for me where I can move through the pages and
  write whats wrong with the screenshot they gave me"* · *"check to see if TO is really doing its
  job of minimizing what the agents have toi read in overhead"* · *"since about a week or so after
  the adoption of V10 I started using Opus 5 at max exclusively, the sonnet tasks where giving
  out too many bugs again … Maybe if we do builder tasks they only modify one or two files at a
  time, smaller edit jobs"* · *"I noticed that agents were using python a lot to modify the
  implementation plan. Maybe we could add that to the new TH task, create the necessary tooling
  for agents to change their handoffs, statuses, etc"* · *"alright I adopt it, perfect. I want
  you to propagate it to the users and write the task blocks right after the last done or in
  progress block."*
  **Testing (§4 Rule 4, §2.2, §3.1, §14.3):** `TH` builds the one harness in the seed lot
  (`TH-adopt` for existing projects); the seven-step routine binds every task — scoped iterate,
  red-arm, plan edits, one full loop last, handoff from that run, late fixes re-verified scoped;
  `TR` measures and fixes loop wall-clock; the loop count is the second metric; human-run tiers
  run per lot. Measured basis: 41 % of full loops redundant in one phase, scoped runs 20× cheaper
  for the same signal; a RED campaign 864 s → 52 s with zero defects opened.
  **Toolkit (annex §A, §1, §11):** `plan.py`, `verify.py`, `launch.py`, `review_page.py`,
  `capture_web.mjs`, `status_page.py`, `scratch_copy.sh`, specified as skeletons every project's
  TH builds from — the lead, 2026-09-16: *"I dont want to have to bring toolkit files with the
  playbook. Id like for it to be self contained. Maybe you can write the toolkit skeleton as an
  annex to the playbook? Let the agent build it from your skeleton?"*; seven projects had each
  rebuilt the runner, six the launchers, seven the capture harness, five the plan checker; ad-hoc
  scripts over plan files ran at ~1,800 per week.
  **Compaction (§2.3, §8, §11):** a header budget (≤600 lines, Pipeline state ≤60) checked by the
  lint fires a BUILDER `TC`; phase-closing V's stub their DONE blocks; Rules become an index with
  `m<N>_rules.md`; TZ authors fresh and carries by citation; the TO gate folds into TB (measured:
  TO cut 7–35 % once and never re-fired; the Pipeline state of one plan regrew from 62 to over
  10,000 lines).
  **Stances and models (§0, §9):** BUILDER/JUDGE are stances; the plan header names the models
  (default Opus for both — what has run every task since 2026-09-05); role mode `MIXED` or
  `ALL-JUDGE` (the lead, 2026-09-06: *"this will give them the authority to make small
  modifications to the contract, the plan, etc, by asking me, but not recommending a bypass
  fix"*); the `E` edit job (≤2 files, ≤150 lines) with an opt-in cheaper-model experiment; the
  override ceremony is one line (the lead, 2026-09-12: *"we override if it looks good. We dont get
  bogged down in bureaucracy with frozen stuff of overrides"*); composite stance tags.
  **Law homes (§1, §15):** one file travels — the lead, 2026-09-16: *"I only want to plop the
  rulebook in a folder and off it goes. Self contained"*, *"no not even claude.md we put everything
  in the playbook. Its meant to be perferctly self contained"* — `CLAUDE.md` is generated from §C,
  identical everywhere; facts, hazards, grants and rules live in the plan; TP verifies the carry.
  **The metric (§2.3, §2.5):** one source of truth (`plan.py dt`), live-planned T as denominator,
  `DBG` counted apart (the lead: *"Include DBG in D:T"*), builder and gates lines, the stop
  trigger as a re-pricing gate the lead may waive without waiving the metric, D's naming the files
  they opened, allowance fallback ≈0.5.
  **Walks (§2.3, §8, E10):** one live `TW` per milestone; `TV` review pages after every surface
  lot; triage collects first, measures once, asks in blocks of ≤4 (the lead, 2026-09-15: *"Wait
  until im done before looking at the code, thats why theres a triage phase"*).
  **Convergent rulings promoted (§3.2, §5 E1, §12, §14.3):** ask in blocks of up to four (four
  repos); `- Status:` first line (two); the finder files the D under three tests and never builds
  it (two); ids minted by grep; the run tag re-derived at handover (*"why are you writing not run,
  when you actually ran it"*); journal entries prefixed by their journal; a walk handed over one
  step at a time; satisfiability checked at freeze.
  **Orchestrator (§7):** O7 a filed block runs next; O8 the two-way status page on the tailnet,
  critical questions halt, polled until the run ends (the lead: *"If he has any question he asks
  them and if its critical he waits for my answer"*).
  **Loosenings recorded per §15, each on the lead's words above:** the one-line override (§9);
  one live walk per milestone with review pages in place of TW-lite and the mid-pipeline TW (§8);
  the TO gate removed (folded into TB); the one-time cross-file grant dropped (covered by
  ALL-JUDGE mode); "Sonnet" removed from the text.
- **v10 — 2026-09-02 — The builder-sized task.** Metric: D tasks per planned T, per phase, with a
  stop trigger and a trend-priced allowance. Sizing by operative tests (one artifact kind, ≤2
  failure classes, no context switch, the ruling-dependency cut, grep-verified file lists, one
  notch smaller) with named exceptions; TG sizes and TB should find nothing; lot cap removed;
  read-load rules (grep to your block, Flow as a table, Pipeline state a register, `.md` caps
  retired); verification tiers PASS / NOT PROVEN (synthetic) / NOT PROVEN (source only), red-armed
  enforcement, every verdict names its box, TW-lite; the plan runnable top to bottom with TW/V
  move-only; `TE-V<N>` as a task type; AskUserQuestion as the channel; run tag above the fence;
  restore by Write/Edit; read-only git allowed; OPT-C lanes; OPT-G client engagement; one canonical
  home with byte-identical copies and the bump ceremony.
- **v9 — 2026-08-12** — fresh baseline: structural length control, run-status labels,
  count-derived verdicts, live walks and journals, direction-change rulings, OPT-D/OPT-A
  hardening, OPT-F sibling tracks, bump-on-edit.
